I mean… I’m still kind of stuck on that. Before this, I can’t really point to a time and say, yeah, that was definitely over two weeks and then it only got better because something changed.

That’s what made me think maybe I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or having a low mood most days, and if so, how long has that been going on?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. It’s been, uh… about two months maybe. It kind of started after that window display award thing at work, when the district manager started paying all that attention to me.\n\nI mean, the display was fine, people made a bigger deal out of it than they needed to, and after that I just… I don’t know, I’ve felt lower most days since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or activities, since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I used to look forward to stuff like gallery nights, or playing basketball, or going to get those food-truck tacos after work. I’d help with kids’ art things sometimes too, and that used to make me feel… I don’t know, useful.\n\nLately I’ll go and I’m just kind of standing there, like I’m not really in it. People are talking and laughing, and I’m thinking about how long I have to stay before it looks rude. Then I usually make some excuse and leave.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling tired or lacking energy a lot of the time, even if you’ve gotten enough sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess so. It’s not like I’m doing heavy lifting all day, but by the middle of a shift my body just feels… heavy. Like even straightening racks, or walking back to the stockroom, feels like it takes way more out of me than it should.\n\nAnd the stockroom is always a mess anyway, like people just throw hangers wherever, and then you’re stepping over boxes trying not to knock anything down… but yeah, I get worn out pretty fast. It feels harder than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not good enough or blamed yourself for things more than usual since this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot, honestly. I keep thinking I didn’t really deserve that award, or the whole supervisor chance thing. Like people saw something that wasn’t actually there.\n\nI know my coworkers say I didn’t trick anybody, that I worked hard on it, but it still feels like… I don’t know, like I fooled everyone somehow. And then I feel guilty, like I took attention or a chance from somebody who actually earned it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times before this where you felt this way for a couple of weeks or more, or has this been the first time you've felt like this for a while?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches before, like everybody does, I guess… where work sucked or I didn’t want to deal with people. Especially around inventory time, because they keep changing how they want things counted and then everyone acts like it’s your fault when the numbers don’t match.\n\nBut… I don’t know if it was like this for weeks before. I don’t really keep track of it like that. This feels like it’s been going on a while, but as far as before-before, I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period before this one where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m having a hard time saying for sure. Like, I’ve had times where I was in a bad mood or didn’t feel like doing much, but I don’t really remember if it was more than two weeks straight.\n\nI wasn’t really counting days, you know? Sometimes work just kind of blurs together, especially when schedules change and you’re closing one night and opening the next. So… maybe? But I can’t say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Before this current time, have you ever had a period where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks without getting better on its own?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t really know. I keep trying to think back and it all kind of blends together. Like there were definitely times I was miserable or didn’t care about much, but whether it was two weeks, or if it got better on its own… I didn’t really measure it like that.\n\nSometimes it was just work stuff, or being tired, or I’d get through a few shifts and then feel kind of okay for a bit. So… I can’t honestly say yes. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before this current time, have you ever had a period where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks that didn’t get better on its own, even after some time passed?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of something that fits that exactly, but… I don’t really know. Like, there were times I was down, sure, but whether it was more than two weeks and didn’t get better on its own… I can’t pin that down.\n\nA lot of times I’d just keep going to work and then one day it’d feel a little less bad, or something would distract me. Like sometimes if the store got busy, you don’t really have time to think, and then you go home and it all comes back, but I don’t know if that counts. So… I’m not saying it never happened. I just don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks that didn’t get better, even after some time passed, and you had to really push through it without things feeling better on their own?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I keep trying to match it to what you’re saying, like “more than two weeks” and “didn’t get better,” but I don’t really have it sorted like that in my head.\n\nThere were times I felt pretty low and just had to get through work, like just show up and do the register and act normal with customers, and then after my shift I’d just be tired. But I don’t know if that was a whole stretch like you mean or if it just came and went.\n\nSometimes it’s hard to tell too, because when you’re working retail, the days kind of run together. Like if inventory was coming up or the manager was changing schedules, everything just felt bad anyway. So… I don’t know. I can’t really say I had a past time like that for sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time in your life before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks, and it didn’t get better unless you really worked at it or something changed externally?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m trying to think of one clear time like that, but nothing comes up where I can say, yeah, that was definitely more than two weeks and it only changed because I did something or something big happened.\n\nI mean, there were times I felt crappy for a while, but then work would get busy, or the schedule would change, or I’d just kind of get through the week and not think about it as much. Retail makes time weird, honestly — like you’re just looking at the next shift, the next paycheck, whether someone called out again.\n\nSo… I can’t really give you a solid yes. I’m not sure I have a time before this that fits exactly what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time in your life before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks, and it only started to improve when something changed in your life or you really had to push through it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say that, not clearly. Like, I get what you’re asking — if there was a stretch before now where it stayed bad and only got better because something changed — but I can’t pull out a clean example.\n\nI mean, life stuff changed all the time, schedules at work, different managers, people quitting and then everyone else covering shifts. Sometimes that kind of thing would make everything feel worse or keep me busy enough that I didn’t notice how I felt. But I don’t know if that means I improved because of it or if I was just distracted.\n\nSo… I’m not trying to dodge it, I just don’t really know. I can’t think of a definite time before this that fits.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks, and it only got better when something changed in your life or you really had to push through it?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m still kind of stuck on that. Before this, I can’t really point to a time and say, yeah, that was definitely over two weeks and then it only got better because something changed.\n\nLike, I’ve had rough patches, sure, but then I start thinking about work stuff — like how if someone quits, suddenly everybody’s schedule changes, and then you’re doing closing shifts you didn’t expect, and it messes up your whole week. So I don’t know if I was actually feeling better or just too busy dealing with that.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I can give you a clear one. I’m not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I can’t think of a definite time before this that fits.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks, and it only got better when something changed in your life or you really had to push through it?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m still kind of stuck on that. Before this, I can’t really point to a time and say, yeah, that was definitely over two weeks and then it only got better because something changed.\n\nLike, I’ve had rough patches, sure, but then I start thinking about work stuff — like how if someone quits, suddenly everybody’s schedule changes, and then you’re doing closing shifts you didn’t expect, and it messes up your whole week. So I don’t know if I was actually feeling better or just too busy dealing with that.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I can give you a clear one. I’m not sure.\n\n[Last Question]\nCan you think of any time before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks straight, and it only started to get better when something changed in your life or you had to really push through it?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 25 / retail_salesperson / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, I know you’re asking the same kind of thing, and I’m trying not to just circle around it. I don’t have a clear time like that before now.

I can remember being in bad moods or just not caring as much about stuff, but then I start mixing it up with, like, work being weird or my hours changing. Sometimes if I got put on a bunch of closing shifts, my whole sleep thing would get thrown off, and then everything felt worse anyway, but I don’t know if that counts as, like, a two-week thing or just life being annoying.

So… I guess my answer is I’m not sure. I can’t really think of a definite period before this where it was more than two weeks straight and then only got better because something changed.

Those thoughts only showed up once I stopped enjoying things and started sleeping and eating badly.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I’m still going to work, but I’ve been messing up inventory counts and display plans enough that my manager has had to double-check me. I’ve also been skipping gallery nights, basketball, and the youth art workshops I used to show up for.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about two months, since the window display award and all the attention from the district manager.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 25 / retail_salesperson / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S026\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Can you think of any time in your life before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks, and it only started to improve when something changed in your life or you really had to push through it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say that, not clearly. Like, I get what you’re asking — if there was a stretch before now where it stayed bad and only got better because something changed — but I can’t pull out a clean example.\n\nI mean, life stuff changed all the time, schedules at work, different managers, people quitting and then everyone else covering shifts. Sometimes that kind of thing would make everything feel worse or keep me busy enough that I didn’t notice how I felt. But I don’t know if that means I improved because of it or if I was just distracted.\n\nSo… I’m not trying to dodge it, I just don’t really know. I can’t think of a definite time before this that fits.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks, and it only got better when something changed in your life or you really had to push through it?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m still kind of stuck on that. I don’t have a clear time like that before now.\n\nI can remember being in bad moods or just not caring as much about stuff, but then I start mixing it up with, like, work being weird or my hours changing. Sometimes if I got put on a bunch of closing shifts, my whole sleep thing would get thrown off, and then everything felt worse anyway, but I don’t know if that counts as, like, a two-week thing or just life being annoying.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I’m not sure. I can’t really think of a definite period before this where it was more than two weeks straight and then only got better because something changed.\n\n[Last Question]\nCan you think of any time before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks straight, and it only started to get better when something changed in your life or you had to really push through it?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I still can’t really come up with one. Like, I know what you mean — more than two weeks straight, and then something changes and it lifts — but before this, I don’t have a clear example.

It kind of started after that window display award thing at work, when the district manager started paying all that attention to me.\n\nI mean, the display was fine, people made a bigger deal out of it than they needed to, and after that I just… I don’t know, I’ve felt lower most days since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or activities, since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I used to look forward to stuff like gallery nights, or playing basketball, or going to get those food-truck tacos after work. People are talking and laughing, and I’m thinking about how long I have to stay before it looks rude. I keep thinking I didn’t really deserve that award, or the whole supervisor chance thing. And then I feel guilty, like I took attention or a chance from somebody who actually earned it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times before this where you felt this way for a couple of weeks or more, or has this been the first time you've felt like this for a while?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t really keep track of it like that. Like, I’ve had times where I was in a bad mood or didn’t feel like doing much, but I don’t really remember if it was more than two weeks straight.\n\nI wasn’t really counting days, you know? Like there were definitely times I was miserable or didn’t care about much, but whether it was two weeks, or if it got better on its own… I didn’t really measure it like that.\n\nSometimes it was just work stuff, or being tired, or I’d get through a few shifts and then feel kind of okay for a bit. So… I can’t honestly say yes. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before this current time, have you ever had a period where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks that didn’t get better on its own, even after some time passed?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of something that fits that exactly, but… I don’t really know. Like, there were times I was down, sure, but whether it was more than two weeks and didn’t get better on its own… I can’t pin that down.\n\nA lot of times I’d just keep going to work and then one day it’d feel a little less bad, or something would distract me. Like sometimes if the store got busy, you don’t really have time to think, and then you go home and it all comes back, but I don’t know if that counts. So… I’m not saying it never happened. I just don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks that didn’t get better, even after some time passed, and you had to really push through it without things feeling better on their own?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I keep trying to match it to what you’re saying, like “more than two weeks” and “didn’t get better,” but I don’t really have it sorted like that in my head.\n\nThere were times I felt pretty low and just had to get through work, like just show up and do the register and act normal with customers, and then after my shift I’d just be tired. But I don’t know if that was a whole stretch like you mean or if it just came and went.\n\nSometimes it’s hard to tell too, because when you’re working retail, the days kind of run together. manifestation: At work I keep losing my place on simple stuff, like counting inventory or picking colors for a display, and I end up second-guessing myself over things I used to do fast.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: Before I started feeling this low after the award attention, I never believed coworkers were building a case against me or that my art was stolen. I’ve also been skipping gallery nights, basketball, and the youth art workshops I used to show up for.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about two months, since the window display award and all the attention from the district manager.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 25 / retail_salesperson / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S026\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Can you think of any time before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks straight, and it only started to get better when something changed in your life or you had to really push through it?\n\nPatient: I still can’t really come up with one. Two months straight? I don’t think I can really say that happened before this. I mean, I’ve had rough patches, but when you say two months, that’s… that’s a long time to know for sure.

Like sometimes if the store got busy, you don’t really have time to think, and then you go home and it all comes back, but I don’t know if that counts. So… I’m not saying it never happened. I just don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in the past when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks that didn’t get better, even after some time passed, and you had to really push through it without things feeling better on their own?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I keep trying to match it to what you’re saying, like “more than two weeks” and “didn’t get better,” but I don’t really have it sorted like that in my head.\n\nThere were times I felt pretty low and just had to get through work, like just show up and do the register and act normal with customers, and then after my shift I’d just be tired. But I don’t know if that was a whole stretch like you mean or if it just came and went.\n\nSometimes it’s hard to tell too, because when you’re working retail, the days kind of run together. Like if inventory was coming up or the manager was changing schedules, everything just felt bad anyway. So… I don’t know. I’m not sure I have a time before this that fits exactly what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time in your life before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks, and it only started to improve when something changed in your life or you really had to push through it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say that, not clearly.
